Continuing work from thread T-019b9eec-b4b2-7369-a1fb-80c1a2dc4d69. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/update/dock.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/view/geometry.rs @src/panel/dock.rs @src/model/editor_area.rs @keymap.yaml @src/messages.rs @src/keymap/command.rs @src/markdown/preview.rs

Other relevant files: src/runtime/app.rs, src/view/hit_test.rs, src/update/preview.rs, src/commands.rs

I just fixed two issues with the dock panel system:

- The keymap.yaml file was missing the dock panel keybindings. I added Cmd+1 for ToggleFileExplorer, Cmd+2 for ToggleTerminal, Cmd+7 for ToggleOutline, and Cmd+B for legacy ToggleSidebar. The user wants me to remove ToggleSidebar since it's legacy.

- I fixed the editor area geometry in src/view/mod.rs render() function to subtract right_dock_width and bottom_dock_height from the available_rect. However, there are still issues:
  - Pressing Cmd+2 or Cmd+7 twice does NOT close the dock panel as expected (FocusOrTogglePanel should toggle on second press)
  - The markdown preview is rendering ON TOP of the dock panels (z-order issue)
  - The dock panels are still not properly affecting the editor group/container area size
  - When command palette is open, markdown preview renders differently (likely damage tracking issue)

Key architecture details I discovered:
- DockMsg::FocusOrTogglePanel(PanelId) is the toggle action dispatched from keybindings
- The dock toggle logic is in src/update/dock.rs
- Markdown preview uses LayoutNode::Preview in the editor_area layout tree and creates splits via wrap_group_with_preview()
- PreviewPane has its own rect that gets set during compute_layout_node()
- Rendering order in render() function: editor_area first, then sidebar, then right dock, then bottom dock, then status bar, then modals
- The damage tracking system in src/view/mod.rs determines what regions get re-rendered (Damage::Full vs partial)
- DockLayout contains left/right/bottom Dock structs with is_open, size_logical, panel_ids, active_panel_idx

The FocusOrTogglePanel logic should:
1. If dock not focused and panel not active, focus dock and activate panel
2. If dock already focused on this panel, close the dock
3. If dock focused but different panel, switch to this panel

Something is wrong with step 2 - the dock doesn't close on second press of the same keybinding.

The user wants me to investigate thoroughly including consulting oracle about the damage tracking and rendering layering issues.

remove togglesidebar since its legacy, note that pressing cmd+2 or 3 two times does not close the dock panel so something must be wrong with it . them arkdown preview is rendering ontop of our dock panels and the dock panels are not affecting the size of the editor group/container area  this is wrong, also when the command palette is open themarkdown preview renders differently, it might be related to damage tracking feature, investigate the coedbase thuroughly, consult oracle.

## Assistant

I see the issue now! The `FocusTarget` enum only has `Editor`, `Sidebar`, and `Modal` - there's no `Dock(DockPosition)` variant for right/bottom docks. This means the `is_dock_focused` closure in `update_dock.rs` always returns `false` for right/bottom docks.

Let me check the render function to understand the dock rendering and layout issues:

}

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_018psuHDiaqLM4pGJwd5bBbS`

```
1) TL;DR

- Add a `FocusTarget::Dock(DockPosition)` variant and wire it into `update_dock.rs` so right/bottom docks can be “focused” and then toggled closed on second keypress.
- `preview.rect` is only computed in `EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled` from the `available_rect` passed in; as long as you use the same “editor area minus docks” rect there (like in `view::render`), previews won’t overlap the docks.
- To avoid subtle mismatches, use a single source of truth (e.g. `DockRects::compute`) for editor-area/dock rectangles in both rendering and hit testing, and ensure dock visibility changes trigger a full redraw.

Effort: M (1–3h) to implement `FocusTarget::Dock` and clean up layout usage; more if you fully integrate `DockRects` everywhere.

## 1. FocusTarget for right/bottom docks (Issue 1)

### a) Extend FocusTarget

In `src/model/ui.rs`, update `FocusTarget` to carry a dock position:

```rust
use crate::panel::DockPosition; // add this

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub enum FocusTarget {
    /// Main editor text area (default)
    #[default]
    Editor,
    /// File tree sidebar (left dock’s file explorer)
    Sidebar,
    /// A dock (right or bottom; left can still use Sidebar for compatibility)
    Dock(DockPosition),
    /// Modal dialog (command palette, goto line, find/replace, etc.)
    Modal,
}
```

Then add small helpers to `UiState` (recommended for clarity and to avoid repeating pattern matches):

```rust
impl UiState {
    // ...

    pub fn focused_dock(&self) -> Option<DockPosition> {
        match self.focus {
            FocusTarget::Dock(pos) => Some(pos),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn focus_dock(&mut self, pos: DockPosition) {
        if self.focus != FocusTarget::Dock(pos) {
            tracing::trace!("Focus changed: {:?} -> Dock({:?})", self.focus, pos);
            self.focus = FocusTarget::Dock(pos);
        }
    }

    // existing focus_editor/focus_sidebar/focus_modal stay as-is
}
```

This keeps left sidebar behavior (`FocusTarget::Sidebar`) intact while allowing proper focus for right and bottom docks.

### b) Fix `FocusOrTogglePanel` behavior in `update_dock.rs`

Now wire focus into the dock update logic.

Current code:

```rust
let is_dock_focused = |pos: DockPosition| -> bool {
    match pos {
        DockPosition::Left => model.ui.focus == FocusTarget::Sidebar,
        // TODO: implement FocusTarget::Dock(pos) for right/bottom
        _ => false,
    }
};
```

Change to:

```rust
let is_dock_focused = |pos: DockPosition| -> bool {
    match pos {
        DockPosition::Left => model.ui.focus == FocusTarget::Sidebar,
        DockPosition::Right | DockPosition::Bottom => {
            model.ui.focus == FocusTarget::Dock(pos)
        }
    }
};
```

Then handle the focus result from `focus_or_toggle_panel`:

```rust
let (opened, position) =
    model.dock_layout.focus_or_toggle_panel(panel_id, is_dock_focused);

// Update focus based on result
if opened {
    if let Some(pos) = position {
        if pos == DockPosition::Left {
            model.ui.focus = FocusTarget::Sidebar;
        } else {
            model.ui.focus = FocusTarget::Dock(pos);
        }
    }
} else {
    model.ui.focus = FocusTarget::Editor;
}
```

With this, the behavior matches the spec:

- First press on a panel keybinding:
  - `is_dock_focused` is false → `focus_or_toggle_panel` opens dock and activates panel → we set focus to that dock.
- Second press (same panel, same dock already focused):
  - `is_dock_focused` is true and dock is open and panel is active → `focus_or_toggle_panel` closes → we move focus back to editor.

### c) Fix other dock-related messages

To make right/bottom docks fully usable, update other `DockMsg` arms.

**CloseFocusedDock:**

```rust
DockMsg::CloseFocusedDock => {
    match model.ui.focus {
        FocusTarget::Sidebar => {
            model.dock_layout.close_dock(DockPosition::Left);
            sync_workspace_with_dock(model);
        }
        FocusTarget::Dock(pos) => {
            model.dock_layout.close_dock(pos);
            // No workspace sync for right/bottom (they’re not the workspace sidebar)
        }
        _ => {}
    }

    model.ui.focus = FocusTarget::Editor;
    Some(Cmd::Redraw)
}
```

**FocusDock(position):**

```rust
DockMsg::FocusDock(position) => {
    model.dock_layout.dock_mut(position).is_open = true;

    if position == DockPosition::Left {
        model.ui.focus = FocusTarget::Sidebar;
        sync_workspace_with_dock(model);
    } else {
        model.ui.focus = FocusTarget::Dock(position);
    }

    Some(Cmd::Redraw)
}
```

**NextPanelInDock / PrevPanelInDock:**

```rust
DockMsg::NextPanelInDock => {
    match model.ui.focus {
        FocusTarget::Sidebar => {
            model.dock_layout.next_panel_in_dock(DockPosition::Left);
        }
        FocusTarget::Dock(pos) => {
            model.dock_layout.next_panel_in_dock(pos);
        }
        _ => {}
    }
    Some(Cmd::Redraw)
}

DockMsg::PrevPanelInDock => {
    match model.ui.focus {
        FocusTarget::Sidebar => {
            model.dock_layout.prev_panel_in_dock(DockPosition::Left);
        }
        FocusTarget::Dock(pos) => {
            model.dock_layout.prev_panel_in_dock(pos);
        }
        _ => {}
    }
    Some(Cmd::Redraw)
}
```

Resizing (`StartResize`/`UpdateResize`) is currently left-only; you can leave the TODOs for now, since they don’t affect the toggle/focus logic.

## 2. Where preview.rect is computed and whether it uses dock sizes (Issues 2 & 3)

### a) Source of `preview.rect`

`preview.rect` is only written in `EditorArea::compute_layout_node`:

```rust
fn compute_layout_node(
    &mut self,
    node: &LayoutNode,
    rect: Rect,
    splitters: &mut Vec<SplitterBar>,
    splitter_width: f32,
) {
    match node {
        LayoutNode::Empty => {}
        LayoutNode::Group(group_id) => {
            if let Some(group) = self.groups.get_mut(group_id) {
                group.rect = rect;
            }
        }
        LayoutNode::Preview(preview_id) => {
            if let Some(preview) = self.previews.get_mut(preview_id) {
                preview.rect = rect;
            }
        }
        LayoutNode::Split(container) => {
            // recursively subdivides `rect` among children...
        }
    }
}
```

and the only public entry point to that is:

```rust
pub fn compute_layout_scaled(
    &mut self,
    available: Rect,
    splitter_width: f32,
) -> Vec<SplitterBar> {
    self.last_layout_rect = Some(available);

    let mut splitters = Vec::new();
    self.compute_layout_node(&self.layout.clone(), available, &mut splitters, splitter_width);
    splitters
}
```

So the *only* thing that controls `preview.rect` is the `available` rect you pass into `compute_layout_scaled`.

### b) How view::render computes that `available_rect`

From your `view::render` snippet:

```rust
let status_bar_height = line_height;

// Sidebar width (left dock / workspace)
let sidebar_width = model
    .workspace
    .as_ref()
    .filter(|ws| ws.sidebar_visible)
    .map(|ws| ws.sidebar_width(model.metrics.scale_factor))
    .unwrap_or(0.0);

// Dock sizes (right + bottom)
let right_dock_width = model.dock_layout.right.size(model.metrics.scale_factor);
let bottom_dock_height = model.dock_layout.bottom.size(model.metrics.scale_factor);

// Height usable above status bar
let content_height = (height as usize).saturating_sub(status_bar_height) as f32;

// Editor area rect = window minus sidebar and right/bottom docks
let available_rect = Rect::new(
    sidebar_width,
    0.0,
    (width as f32) - sidebar_width - right_dock_width,
    content_height - bottom_dock_height,
);

// This is what drives group.rect and preview.rect
let splitters = model
    .editor_area
    .compute_layout_scaled(available_rect, model.metrics.splitter_width);
```

That means:

- All groups and previews live strictly inside:
  - x: `[sidebar_width, width - right_dock_width)`
  - y: `[0, content_height - bottom_dock_height)`

So **by construction**, `preview.rect` cannot extend into the right/bottom dock regions unless:

- `right_dock_width` or `bottom_dock_height` are computed incorrectly, or
- some other code calls `compute_layout` or `compute_layout_scaled` using a different `available` rect, overwriting `preview.rect` with stale/full-window coordinates.

The code you pasted for `view::render` is the canonical call; if there are older callers (e.g., in hit-testing or update code) that still use a pre-dock `available_rect`, they might temporarily set `preview.rect` wrong, but the next `view::render` will overwrite it with the correct rect.

Given your current snippet, though, the *rendering* path is correct and consistent with the dock sizes.

### c) Render order vs “preview on top of docks”

The render order is:

1. Render editor area (groups + previews + splitters):

   ```rust
   Self::render_editor_area(&mut frame, &mut painter, model, &splitters);
   ```

2. Render sidebar (left file explorer)
3. If open, render right dock:

   ```rust
   let dock_rect = Rect::new(
       width as f32 - right_width,
       0.0,
       right_width,
       height as f32 - status_bar_height as f32 - bottom_height,
   );
   Self::render_dock(... DockPosition::Right, dock_rect);
   ```

4. If open, render bottom dock:

   ```rust
   let dock_rect = Rect::new(
       sidebar_width,
       height as f32 - status_bar_height as f32 - bottom_height,
       width as f32 - sidebar_width - model.dock_layout.right.size(model.metrics.scale_factor),
       bottom_height,
   );
   Self::render_dock(... DockPosition::Bottom, dock_rect);
   ```

So docks are drawn *after* previews and should visually sit **on top** wherever they overlap in x/y. If you see markdown visibly “on top” of the docks, what’s almost certainly happening is:

- The preview is correctly confined to the editor area (`available_rect`),
- But the *damaged* region doesn’t include the dock area, so when the dock visibility changes, the dock region isn’t fully redrawn, and old preview pixels are still visible in the back buffer.

That’s where your command-palette behavior is relevant:

- When a modal is active, `compute_effective_damage` forces `Damage::Full`:

  ```rust
  if model.ui.active_modal.is_some() {
      return Damage::Full;
  }
  ```

  so you get a total clear (`frame.clear(bg_color)`) plus a full re-render, and there are no stale pixels.

- When only dock state changes, unless the runtime maps `DockMsg` to `Damage::Full`, you may be getting partial damage that doesn’t cover the dock rect. The old preview area in that region then peeks through.

So the difference you’re seeing between “command palette + preview” and “docks + preview” is almost certainly **damage-region vs dock rect mismatch**, not the preview layout itself.

---

## 3. Other places where dock sizes/layout matter

Given the current design, there are three key “geometry sources” that should stay in sync:

1. **Editor area & previews** – via `EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled(available_rect, ...)`
2. **Dock areas** – via either:
   - The explicit rects calculated in `view::render` (right/bottom), and
   - `geometry::DockRects::compute` (currently unused).
3. **Hit-testing & input routing** – anything that needs to know “is the click in a dock / editor / preview / resize handle?”

### a) Single source of truth: DockRects

You already have a good abstraction in `geometry::DockRects`:

```rust
pub struct DockRects {
    pub left: Option<Rect>,
    pub right: Option<Rect>,
    pub bottom: Option<Rect>,
    pub editor_area: Rect,
}

impl DockRects {
    pub fn compute(
        dock_layout: &DockLayout,
        window_width: u32,
        window_height: u32,
        status_bar_height: usize,
        scale_factor: f64,
    ) -> Self { /* ... */ }
}
```

It uses:

```rust
let left_width = dock_layout.left.size(scale_factor);
let right_width = dock_layout.right.size(scale_factor);
let bottom_height = dock_layout.bottom.size(scale_factor);

// side_dock_height = content_height - bottom_height
// editor_area: Rect::new(left_width, 0.0, w - left_width - right_width, side_dock_height)
```

This matches the logic you’re hand-writing in `view::render`, except you also have the *workspace* sidebar (`sidebar_width`) which is logically the left dock for Explorer.

**Recommended minimal integration:**

- In `view::render`, instead of hand-computing `available_rect` and dock rects, do:

  ```rust
  let line_height = self.line_height();
  let status_bar_height = line_height;
  let (width, height) = (self.width, self.height);

  // Workspace sidebar (still a separate concept)
  let sidebar_width = model
      .workspace
      .as_ref()
      .filter(|ws| ws.sidebar_visible)
      .map(|ws| ws.sidebar_width(model.metrics.scale_factor))
      .unwrap_or(0.0);

  let dock_rects = geometry::DockRects::compute(
      &model.dock_layout,
      width,
      height,
      status_bar_height,
      model.metrics.scale_factor,
  );

  // EditorArea available rect = editor_area but shifted by workspace sidebar
  let mut available_rect = dock_rects.editor_area;
  available_rect.x += sidebar_width;
  available_rect.width -= sidebar_width;

  let splitters = model
      .editor_area
      .compute_layout_scaled(available_rect, model.metrics.splitter_width);
  ```

- Then use `dock_rects.right` / `dock_rects.bottom` for dock rendering (and adjust their x to account for sidebar if you decide left “dock” and workspace sidebar should be unified later).

This ensures:

- Any future change to dock layout (min sizes, max fractions, etc.) is centralized in `DockRects::compute`.
- Editor area, previews, and docks always share the same geometric basis.

### b) Damage vs docks

To avoid the “preview ghosting behind docks” problem:

- Ensure that any message that changes dock visibility or active panel (e.g., `FocusOrTogglePanel`, `ToggleDock`, `CloseFocusedDock`, `FocusDock`) causes a **full redraw** or at least marks both:
  - The entire editor area, and
  - All dock rectangles,

  as damaged.

In your current `update_dock.rs`, all those arms return `Some(Cmd::Redraw)`. How that maps to `Damage` depends on your runtime; the safest short-term change is:

- Make `Cmd::Redraw` map to `Damage::Full` (or add a `Cmd::RedrawFull` used by dock mutations).
- Or, in damage computation, treat dock-layout changes as requiring full redraw (similar to modals).

This will bring dock behavior in line with the modal behavior you already have (Issue 3) and eliminate stale pixels from old preview positions.

---

## 4. Risks and guardrails

- **Changing FocusTarget is invasive**: there may be other places matching on `FocusTarget` that don’t yet handle `Dock(_)`. Use a quick search on `FocusTarget::` / `match model.ui.focus` and:
  - Decide explicit behavior for `Dock(_)` (usually treat like `Editor` or ignore for now),
  - Or explicitly log unsupported cases so they’re easy to find later.
- **Workspace sidebar vs left dock**: Right now left dock == workspace sidebar conceptually, but they are separate code paths. Keeping `FocusTarget::Sidebar` for left and `FocusTarget::Dock` only for right/bottom is the least risky incremental step.
- **DockRects integration**: Don’t switch all geometry/hit-testing to `DockRects` in one huge diff; start with `view::render` only, verify layout visually, then gradually update hit testing.

---

## 5. When to consider the “advanced” path

Revisit a more complex design if:

- You add many dock types, or docks can host complex nested layouts (e.g., multiple panels stacked/split within a dock).
- You introduce keyboard-navigation across docks (e.g., cycling focus across left/right/bottom, previews, and editor) and want consistent, centralized focus management.
- You start doing fine-grained damage tracking for docks themselves (individual panel content diffing), where correctness of geometry/damage math really matters.

---

## 6. Optional advanced path (brief)

- Unify left “sidebar” and left dock: treat the file explorer as just a panel (`PanelId::FileExplorer`) and make left sidebar drawing go through `render_dock` instead of special casing.
- Use `UiState::focus` with only two high-level variants: `EditorArea` and `Dock(DockPosition)`, and treat specific widgets (sidebar tree, preview, etc.) as sub-focuses internal to those regions.
- Attach dock focus and resizing state to a generalized “dock interaction state” (similar to `SplitterDragState`) and use `DockRects::hit_test_resize` for dock resizing everywhere.

For your current goals, though, the simple changes above (add `FocusTarget::Dock`, wire in `update_dock`, confirm `available_rect` usage, and align damage with dock changes) are enough to fix the observed issues without overhauling the architecture.
```

## Assistant

The dock operations already return `Cmd::Redraw` which maps to `Damage::Full`. The issue is likely that the preview is being rendered with stale rect coordinates. Looking at the code flow again:

1. `render()` computes `available_rect` correctly (with dock sizes subtracted)
2. `compute_layout_scaled(available_rect)` sets `preview.rect` correctly
3. Then previews are rendered within `render_editor_area()`
4. Then docks are rendered on top

The issue might be that when docks open/close, if partial damage is used instead of full, stale pixels from the preview remain. But `Cmd::Redraw` → `Damage::Full` should handle this.

1) TL;DR

- Add a separate `DockMsg::TogglePanel(PanelId)` that ignores focus and use it for command-palette “Toggle …” commands; keep `FocusOrTogglePanel` for keybindings.
- Centralize dock geometry with `geometry::DockRects::compute` and adjust the bottom dock to span under the right dock from the sidebar to the window edge.
- Ensure markdown preview layout is always recomputed from the same dock/editor-area rects and isn’t using stale or differently-computed geometry.

---

## 1. Command palette toggle semantics

### Problem recap

- `DockMsg::FocusOrTogglePanel` delegates to `DockLayout::focus_or_toggle_panel`, which uses an `is_dock_focused` closure that checks `model.ui.focus`.
- When invoked from the command palette, `model.ui.focus` is on the modal, so `is_dock_focused` is always `false` → it always “open+focus” instead of true toggle behavior.
- You want:
  - **Keybinding (Cmd+2, etc.):** VS Code–style “focus-or-toggle”:
    - If dock is not focused → open+focus+switch panel.
    - If dock is focused on that panel → close and return focus to editor.
  - **Command palette:** true toggle independent of focus:
    - If panel is open and active in its dock → close.
    - Else → open+activate.
    - Focus probably left alone (or at least not coupled to “is dock focused”).

### Recommended simple approach (S: <1h)

Introduce a new dock message and a trivial helper in `DockLayout`:

1. **Add a new message variant** for pure toggle:

```rust
// crate::messages::DockMsg
pub enum DockMsg {
    FocusOrTogglePanel(PanelId),
    TogglePanel(PanelId),          // NEW
    CloseFocusedDock,
    FocusDock(DockPosition),
    ToggleDock(DockPosition),
    // ...
}
```

2. **Implement `TogglePanel` handling in `update_dock`** (focus-agnostic):

```rust
pub fn update_dock(model: &mut AppModel, msg: DockMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {
    match msg {
        // existing FocusOrTogglePanel stays exactly as-is
        DockMsg::FocusOrTogglePanel(panel_id) => {
            // ... existing code ...
        }

        DockMsg::TogglePanel(panel_id) => {
            // Find which dock this panel lives in
            if let Some(position) = model.dock_layout.find_panel(panel_id) {
                let dock = model.dock_layout.dock_mut(position);
                let is_active = dock.active_panel() == Some(panel_id);

                if is_active && dock.is_open {
                    // Panel is currently active and visible → close the dock
                    dock.close();
                } else {
                    // Otherwise ensure dock is open and this panel is active
                    dock.activate(panel_id);
                }

                // Keep focus semantics decoupled from palette invocations:
                // - Don't overwrite model.ui.focus here.
                // - Command palette code is responsible for closing the modal
                //   and restoring previous focus (usually the editor).

                if position == DockPosition::Left {
                    // Keep workspace sidebar state in sync
                    sync_workspace_with_dock(model);
                }

                Some(Cmd::Redraw)
            } else {
                None
            }
        }

        // ... rest unchanged ...
    }
}
```

3. **Wire keybindings vs command palette differently:**

- **Keybindings (Cmd+1, Cmd+2, Cmd+7):** keep using `DockMsg::FocusOrTogglePanel(…)` so they preserve the current “focus-based toggle” semantics.

- **Command palette `CommandId::ToggleFileExplorer / ToggleTerminal / ToggleOutline`:**
  In the place where command palette commands are turned into messages (likely in `commands.rs` or a `dispatch_command`/`execute_command` function), change these to use the new message:

```rust
match command_id {
    CommandId::ToggleFileExplorer => {
        Msg::Dock(DockMsg::TogglePanel(PanelId::FILE_EXPLORER))
    }
    CommandId::ToggleTerminal => {
        Msg::Dock(DockMsg::TogglePanel(PanelId::TERMINAL))
    }
    CommandId::ToggleOutline => {
        Msg::Dock(DockMsg::TogglePanel(PanelId::OUTLINE))
    }
    // ...
}
```

Whereas the keymap path (coming from `KeymapCommand`) should still use `FocusOrTogglePanel`.

This cleanly separates semantics:

- **Keymap path:** focus-aware, VS Code–like “focus or toggle”.
- **Palette path:** true “toggle panel by ID, ignoring focus”.

### Why this is the cleanest

- No need to thread “source” (palette vs keymap) through your entire update pipeline.
- Keeps `DockLayout::focus_or_toggle_panel` and `is_dock_focused` closure exactly for what they were designed: keybinding semantics.
- `TogglePanel` is intuitive and composable; you’ll want it later for other triggers (e.g., menu items, scripting).

### Alternative (not recommended now)

You could make `FocusOrTogglePanel` context-aware by pretending some dock is “focused” even when a modal is active (e.g., using a “previous focus” stored in `UiState`). That’s more complex, harder to reason about, and still doesn’t give the simple “regardless of focus” behavior you described for palette commands.

---

## 2. Correct dock geometry (VS Code / IntelliJ style)

### Current bug

From the old `view/mod.rs` code you quoted:

```rust
// Right dock
let dock_rect = Rect::new(
    width as f32 - right_width,
    0.0,
    right_width,
    height as f32 - status_bar_height as f32 - bottom_height,
);

// Bottom dock
let dock_rect = Rect::new(
    sidebar_width,
    height as f32 - status_bar_height as f32 - bottom_height,
    width as f32 - sidebar_width - model.dock_layout.right.size(...),
    bottom_height,
);
```

Problems:

- Right dock is fine vertically, but…
- Bottom dock subtracts the right dock width: `width - sidebar_width - right_width`, so it **stops left of the right dock**, leaving:
  - A vertical strip to the right of bottom dock (empty space).
  - When bottom is closed, right dock covers full height; when bottom opens, bottom only spans under the editor area, not under the right dock.

### Single source of truth: use `geometry::DockRects`

You *already* have a central geometry implementation in `geometry.rs`:

```rust
pub struct DockRects {
    pub left: Option<Rect>,
    pub right: Option<Rect>,
    pub bottom: Option<Rect>,
    pub editor_area: Rect,
}

impl DockRects {
    pub fn compute(
        dock_layout: &DockLayout,
        window_width: u32,
        window_height: u32,
        status_bar_height: usize,
        scale_factor: f64,
    ) -> Self {
        let w = window_width as f32;
        let content_height = (window_height as usize).saturating_sub(status_bar_height) as f32;

        let left_width = dock_layout.left.size(scale_factor);
        let right_width = dock_layout.right.size(scale_factor);
        let bottom_height = dock_layout.bottom.size(scale_factor);

        // Docks + editor area live in `side_dock_height`
        let side_dock_height = content_height - bottom_height;

        let left = if left_width > 0.0 {
            Some(Rect::new(0.0, 0.0, left_width, side_dock_height))
        } else {
            None
        };

        let right = if right_width > 0.0 {
            Some(Rect::new(w - right_width, 0.0, right_width, side_dock_height))
        } else {
            None
        };

        let bottom = if bottom_height > 0.0 {
            Some(Rect::new(
                left_width,
                side_dock_height,
                w - left_width - right_width,   // <-- needs change
                bottom_height,
            ))
        } else {
            None
        };

        let editor_area = Rect::new(
            left_width,
            0.0,
            w - left_width - right_width,
            side_dock_height,
        );

        Self { left, right, bottom, editor_area }
    }
}
```

The **comment** says:

> 1. Left/right docks span full height (minus status bar)
> 2. Bottom dock spans between left and right docks

But the **desired behavior** from your description is:

- Left dock: full height (top to above bottom dock / status bar).
- Right dock: full height over the editor area (also top to above bottom dock).
- Bottom dock: spans *under* the right dock, from the sidebar to the window edge (i.e., do **not** stop left of the right dock).

That implies:

- **Horizontally:**
  - Left dock: `[0 .. left_width)`
  - Editor: `[left_width .. w - right_width)`
  - Right dock: `[w - right_width .. w)`
  - Bottom dock **under both editor and right dock**: `[left_width .. w)`

### Minimal correction (M: 1–3h including integration)

1. **Fix `DockRects::compute` bottom width**:

Change:

```rust
let bottom = if bottom_height > 0.0 {
    Some(Rect::new(
        left_width,
        side_dock_height,
        w - left_width - right_width,
        bottom_height,
    ))
} else {
    None
};
```

To:

```rust
let bottom = if bottom_height > 0.0 {
    Some(Rect::new(
        left_width,
        side_dock_height,
        w - left_width,      // spans under right dock
        bottom_height,
    ))
} else {
    None
};
```

This satisfies:

- When right dock open:
  - Right dock: full height over editor region (`side_dock_height`).
  - Bottom dock: from left dock to window edge, under the right dock area.
- When right dock closed:
  - Right width is 0, so bottom width becomes full width from `left_width` to `w`.

2. **Use `DockRects` for rendering instead of ad-hoc math in `view/mod.rs`:**

Where you currently do something like:

```rust
let right_width = model.dock_layout.right.size(renderer.scale_factor());
let bottom_height = model.dock_layout.bottom.size(renderer.scale_factor());
let sidebar_width = ...;

let right_dock_rect = Rect::new(...); // manual math
let bottom_dock_rect = Rect::new(...);
let available_rect = Rect::new(sidebar_width, 0.0, ..., ...);
```

Replace with:

```rust
let (width, height) = renderer.dimensions();
let line_height = renderer.line_height();
let status_bar_height = geometry::status_bar_height(line_height);

let dock_rects = geometry::DockRects::compute(
    &model.dock_layout,
    width,
    height,
    status_bar_height,
    renderer.scale_factor(),
);

// Use dock_rects.left / .right / .bottom for dock rendering
if let Some(rect) = &dock_rects.right {
    // render right dock using *rect
}
if let Some(rect) = &dock_rects.bottom {
    // render bottom dock using *rect
}

// Use dock_rects.editor_area for editor & preview layout
let available_rect = dock_rects.editor_area;
EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled(available_rect, ...);
```

By doing this:

- You ensure **zero divergence** between dock rects and editor area rects.
- The empty strips under right dock or to the right of bottom dock disappear because both are derived from the identical geometry.

---

## 3. Markdown preview positioning

### What should happen

You compute:

```rust
let available_rect = Rect::new(
    sidebar_width,
    0.0,
    (width as f32) - sidebar_width - right_dock_width,
    content_height - bottom_dock_height,
);

// Then:
EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled(available_rect);
```

Assuming:

- `sidebar_width` is the left dock width in **physical pixels**.
- `right_dock_width` & `bottom_dock_height` are also in physical pixels (`Dock::size(scale_factor)`).
- `content_height` is `height - status_bar_height`.

Then `available_rect` is exactly the editor area rect that `DockRects::editor_area` would compute with the semantics above. The markdown preview pane is then rendered in `render_markdown_preview` using its `rect` and `Pane::with_header(rect, ...)`.

So where can it go wrong?

### Likely failure modes

1. **Stale layout (not recomputed when docks change)**

If `EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled(available_rect)` is not called every time:

- The bottom or right dock sizes change (`Dock.size_logical` or `is_open` toggled).
- Or the window is resized.

Then the stored layout inside `EditorArea` (which includes the preview `rect`) may be based on an *old* `available_rect` from before the dock change.

Symptoms you’d see:

- Open terminal → bottom dock appears, but preview still spans the old, taller area (overlapping the terminal).
- Open right dock → preview does not shrink horizontally and draws under/behind the right dock.

**Fix:**

- Treat dock layout changes as layout-affecting and recompute editor-area layout **on every render** or at least on every `DockMsg` that toggles/open/closes or resizes a dock.

Concretely:

- In the top-level render function where you know current `DockRects::editor_area`, always call `EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled(dock_rects.editor_area)` before rendering editor groups and previews. Do not rely on it being up-to-date from some earlier event.

2. **Two different geometry computations (render vs layout)**

Right now you have:

- Ad-hoc rect computation in `view/mod.rs` (the snippet with `width - sidebar_width - right_width` etc.).
- `geometry::DockRects::compute` (with slightly different semantics).

If the preview’s `available_rect` uses *one* of those formulas and dock rendering uses the other, you’ll get mismatches even if both formulas are “individually correct”.

**Fix:**

- As above: use `DockRects::compute` as the *only* source of dock+editor-area rectangles.
- Pass `dock_rects.editor_area` to `EditorArea::compute_layout_scaled`.
- Render docks using `dock_rects.left/right/bottom`.

Then preview is guaranteed to be aligned with docks.

3. **Unit mismatch: logical vs physical pixels**

You are mixing:

- `Dock::size(scale_factor)` which returns *physical* pixels.
- `size_logical` stored in the dock.
- Possibly `sidebar_width` (if it’s actually stored as logical and not scaled before use).

If `available_rect` uses logical units while docks use physical (or vice versa), at non-1.0 scale factors the preview will be offset or sized incorrectly relative to the docks.

Check:

- `sidebar_width` is likely `workspace.sidebar_width_logical` synced in `sync_workspace_with_dock` from `Dock.size_logical`. When you render, you must multiply by `scale_factor` to get physical pixels, the coordinate space that `Frame` and `Rect` use.

So for a correct `available_rect`:

```rust
let sidebar_width_px = model
    .dock_layout
    .left
    .size(renderer.scale_factor()); // physical

let right_dock_width_px = model
    .dock_layout
    .right
    .size(renderer.scale_factor());

let bottom_dock_height_px = model
    .dock_layout
    .bottom
    .size(renderer.scale_factor());

let content_height = height as f32 - status_bar_height as f32;

let available_rect = Rect::new(
    sidebar_width_px,
    0.0,
    (width as f32) - sidebar_width_px - right_dock_width_px,
    content_height - bottom_dock_height_px,
);
```

Or simply:

```rust
let dock_rects = DockRects::compute(...);
let available_rect = dock_rects.editor_area;
```

Using `DockRects` bypasses the risk of forgetting to apply scale factor consistently.

4. **Z-order / draw order**

If you draw preview **after** docks:

- It can visually overlap the bottom/right docks even if the geometry is correct.
- This might look like “preview not respecting docks” while the math is actually fine.

You want a draw order like:

1. Clear background.
2. Draw docks (left/right/bottom).
3. Draw editor area (groups, text).
4. Draw previews inside editor area.
5. Draw overlays / modals.

If currently you render the editor area (including previews) before the docks, the docks will cover them (which is usually fine). If previews are rendered after docks, they may obscure the terminal/outline, which is incorrect for a side-pane style preview. Double-check `Renderer::render` order.

Given the code snippet, markdown preview is rendered as part of editor group rendering and likely after docks; unifying geometry and correct z-order will resolve overlaps.

---

## 4. Risks and guardrails

- **Behavior divergence between keybindings and palette:**
  Intentional here; document it: keybindings are “focus-aware toggle”, palette is “state toggle”. If user complaints suggest confusion, you might later unify semantics (e.g., always true toggle).

- **Geometry drift between modules:**
  Once you adopt `DockRects::compute`, remove or refactor ad-hoc geometry so nobody can accidentally reintroduce drift.

- **Scale factor bugs:**
  Ensure all geometry uses physical pixels consistently. A quick test at non-1.0 DPI (e.g., 1.25x, 2x) will reveal any mismatches.

---

## 5. When to consider the advanced path

Revisit design if:

- You introduce more complex docking (floating panels, nested docks, or drag-to-reposition).
- You add persistent layouts per workspace and need more sophisticated persistence + migration.
- You need animated transitions where layout is frame-by-frame interpolated.

Then you might want:

- A dedicated layout manager type that owns:
  - DockRects
  - Editor area rects
  - Status bar rect
  - Modal rects
- And reuses it across hit-testing, rendering, and input.

---

## 6. Optional advanced path (outline only)

If you go more advanced later:

- Add a `LayoutContext` struct:

```rust
pub struct LayoutContext {
    pub docks: DockRects,
    pub status_bar_rect: Rect,
    pub modal_rect: Option<Rect>,
    // maybe per-group editor rects, etc.
}
```

- Compute `LayoutContext::compute(model, renderer)` once per frame.
- Pass it down to:
  - Rendering (`Renderer::render_*`).
  - Hit-testing (`hit_test_*` functions).
- This guarantees one geometry definition across the entire app (true Elm-style “model → view” purity).

For now, implementing `DockMsg::TogglePanel`, using `DockRects::compute` everywhere, and ensuring you recompute editor-area layout on dock changes will solve your three issues with minimal, targeted changes.

## Assistant

Here's a summary of all the changes I made:

### Summary of Changes

#### 1. Fixed Dock Panel Toggle Issues

**Added `DockMsg::TogglePanel`** in [src/messages.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/messages.rs#L530-L533) - A focus-agnostic toggle that opens if closed, closes if open, regardless of focus state.

**Implemented handler** in [src/update/dock.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/dock.rs#L51-L76) - Pure open/close toggle for command palette use.

**Semantic distinction:**
- **Keybindings (Cmd+1, Cmd+2, Cmd+7)**: Use `FocusOrTogglePanel` - focus-aware, VS Code-style behavior
- **Command palette**: Uses `TogglePanel` - pure toggle regardless of focus

#### 2. Fixed Command Palette Toggle Commands

Updated [src/update/app.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/app.rs#L305-L317) to use `DockMsg::TogglePanel` for command palette commands instead of `FocusOrTogglePanel`.

#### 3. Fixed Dock Geometry

**Bottom dock now spans full width** - Updated [src/view/geometry.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L913-L922) and [src/view/mod.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L2549-L2553) so the bottom dock extends from sidebar edge to window right edge (under the right dock), eliminating empty space.

#### 4. Removed Legacy Code

- Removed `Cmd+B -> ToggleSidebar` from [keymap.yaml](file://~/code/token-editor/keymap.yaml) (already done)
- Removed `Cmd+B -> ToggleSidebar` from [src/keymap/defaults.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/keymap/defaults.rs#L186-L188)
- Kept `Command::ToggleSidebar` for backwards compatibility with user keymaps (maps to same action as `ToggleFileExplorer`)

#### 5. Added `FocusTarget::Dock(DockPosition)` (from previous work)

This enables proper focus tracking for right/bottom docks, making the keybinding toggle work correctly.
